• Community-aware prototypes as a replay-free memory. To our <sup>k</sup>now<sup>l</sup>edge, CoPE-CGL is t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>fi</sup>rst rep<sup>l</sup>ay-<sup>f</sup>ree CGL method to use <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h communities as the unit of task <sub>memory,</sub> di<sub>s</sub>tilli<sub>ng eac</sub>h t<sub>as</sub>k i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>o a</sub>t <sub>mos</sub>t � <sub>non-</sub>l<sub>earne</sub>d commun<sup>i</sup>t<sub>y</sub>-<sup>l</sup>eve<sup>l</sup> <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>es w<sup>i</sup>t<sup>h</sup> a constant memor<sub>y</sub> cost <sub>p</sub>er tas<sup>k</sup>.

• Fast model maintenance at equal storage. CoPE-CGL runs end to end 1.3–7.9× faster than the most accurate <sub>compe</sub>ti<sub>ng</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>ne on eac</sub>h <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e seven</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s an</sub>d 1.1–14.0× faster in each of the 35 mixed confi<sub>g</sub>urations (seven benchmarks, five task ratios; Sec. 3.4, Secs. 4.2.1– 4.2.2). It also scales to the lar<sub>g</sub>est benchmark (Sec. 4.6). At <sub>equa</sub>l <sub>s</sub>t<sub>orage an</sub>d <sub>w</sub>ith<sub>ou</sub>t t<sub>as</sub>k id<sub>en</sub>tifi<sub>ers,</sub> it <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h<sub>es</sub> th<sub>e</sub> AP th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e s</sub>tr<sub>o</sub>n<sub>ges</sub>t r<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>ay</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>lin<sub>e,</sub> C<sub>a</sub>T<sub>,</sub> r<sub>eac</sub>h<sub>es o</sub>nl<sub>y</sub> with the true task identifier (within 0.89 <sub>p</sub>ercenta<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>oints) while runnin<sub>g</sub> 5.9–14.0× faster (Sec. 4.3).

![](images/95c088aa70c86ede919cb5d8daac3b165a7fd4fa5eb59089dac08f618b5b3acb.jpg)  
Figure 1: Overview of CoPE-CGL. During training, �-hop Laplacian smoothing maps each task graph to $Z _ { b a s e s }$ , on which a task-specific bottleneck adapter is trained and from which community-aware prototypes $\mathcal { P } ^ { t }$ are extracted into memory. During inference, $Z _ { t e s t }$ is routed to the matching adapter and classifier via graph-level majority voting over the prototype memory, or node by node in a node-level variant.

## 3.1 Topology-Infused Bottleneck Adaptation

Motivation. Traditiona<sup>l</sup> GNNs coup<sup>l</sup>e <sup>f</sup>eature trans<sup>f</sup>ormation wit<sup>h</sup> nei<sub>g</sub>hborhood a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ation<sub>,</sub> forcin<sub>g</sub> ex<sub>p</sub>ensive messa<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>assin<sub>g</sub> at ever<sub>y</sub> e<sub>p</sub>och<sub>;</sub> this re<sub>p</sub>eated <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>ation is <sub>p</sub>rohibitive in CGL’s task-<sub>s</sub>t<sub>ream se</sub>tti<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>a source o</sub>f <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>t<sub>ura</sub>l di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>or</sub>ti<sub>on across</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k<sub>s.</sub> CoPE-CGL therefore decou<sub>p</sub>les <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>ation from o<sub>p</sub>timization b<sub>y</sub> followin<sub>g</sub> Sim<sub>p</sub>lified Gra<sub>p</sub>h Convolution (SGC) [41]: it makes multi ho<sub>p</sub> to<sub>p</sub>olo<sub>gy</sub> a <sub>p</sub>arameter-free <sub>p</sub>rior and trains a li<sub>g</sub>htwei<sub>g</sub>ht tasks<sub>p</sub>ecific ada<sub>p</sub>ter on to<sub>p</sub> of it.

Rather than <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>atin<sub>g</sub> messa<sub>g</sub>es inside the trainin<sub>g</sub> loo<sub>p,</sub> we <sub>p</sub>recom<sub>p</sub>ute the �-ho<sub>p</sub> a<sub>g</sub>- <sub>g</sub>r<sub>ega</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n <sub>o</sub>n<sub>ce</sub> <sub>v</sub>i<sub>a</sub> � <sub>spa</sub>r<sub>se</sub> m<sub>a</sub>trix–m<sub>a</sub>trix m<sub>u</sub>lti<sub>p</sub>li<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>o</sub>n<sub>s,</sub>

\mathrm { M e m } ( T ) \leq 4 T \Bigl [ ( 2 d b + b + d ) + ( d n _ { c } + n _ { c } ) + M d \Bigr ] \mathrm { b y t e s } ,\tag{8}
$$

4.1.2 Comparison Methods and Evaluation Metrics. We compare C<sub>o</sub>PE-CGL <sub>aga</sub>in<sub>s</sub>t <sub>e</sub>l<sub>eve</sub>n b<sub>ase</sub>lin<sub>es</sub> <sub>spa</sub>nnin<sub>g</sub> thr<sub>ee</sub> CGL f<sub>a</sub>milies, namel<sub>y</sub> (i) three re<sub>g</sub>ularization-based (EWC [17], MAS [1], TWP [20]), (ii) four re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>-based (ER-GNN [52], SSM [48], CaT [22], TACO [13]), and (iii) four <sub>p</sub>arameter-isolated re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>-free (TPP [25], TAAM [21], CAGNN [42], SAFER [45]). We do not com<sub>p</sub>are with Pi-GNN [44] and HPNs [49], whose <sub>p</sub>arameter-isolation <sub>an</sub>d <sub>pro</sub>t<sub>o</sub>t<sub>ype-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d d<sub>es</sub>i<sub>gns</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>our</sub> <sub>more</sub> <sub>recen</sub>t <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ay-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes a</sub>b<sub>ove.</sub> W<sub>e a</sub>l<sub>so</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>d<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>ne-</sub>t<sub>un</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> as a <sup>l</sup>ower <sup>b</sup>oun<sup>d</sup> an<sup>d</sup> joint training as an o<sup>fl</sup>ine re<sup>f</sup>erence. We assess <sub>p</sub>erformance usin<sub>g</sub> two standard metrics [4]: AP↑ and BWT↑; formal definitions a<sub>pp</sub>ear in A<sub>pp</sub>endix A.2. We additionall<sub>y</sub> <sub>repor</sub>t <sub>wa</sub>ll<sub>-c</sub>l<sub>oc</sub>k <sub>run</sub>ti<sub>me</sub> f<sub>rom mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l <sub>cons</sub>t<sub>ruc</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e</sub> fi<sub>na</sub>l <sub>eva</sub>l<sub>ua</sub>ti<sub>on,</sub> i<sub>nc</sub>l<sub>u</sub>di<sub>ng eac</sub>h <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d’<sub>s own preprocess</sub>i<sub>ng.</sub> All <sub>resu</sub>lt<sub>s</sub> are the mean ± standard deviation across three inde<sub>p</sub>endent runs; <sub>a repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>an</sub>d<sub>ar</sub>d d<sub>ev</sub>i<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on o</sub>f 0<sub>.</sub>00 d<sub>eno</sub>t<sub>es a va</sub>l<sub>ue</sub> b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow</sub> 0<sub>.</sub>005<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d A<sub>ppen</sub>di<sub>x</sub> A<sub>.</sub>2 d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>a</sub>il<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e see</sub>d<sub>s.</sub>

4.1.3 Implementation. Baselines are run with the CaT [22] codebase (built on CGLB [47]) or with the oficial code of each method, <sub>p</sub>orted into the same <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>eline<sub>,</sub> usin<sub>g</sub> each <sub>p</sub>a<sub>p</sub>er’s o<sub>p</sub>timal confi<sub>g</sub>- uration. CoPE-CGL uses one untuned confi<sub>g</sub>uration for all bench-<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s an</sub>d <sub>se</sub>tti<sub>ngs</sub> $( K { = } 2 , M { = } 1 0 , \alpha { = } 1 )$ , with bottleneck width � = min(max(⌊�/4⌋, 16), 128). All baselines exce<sub>p</sub>t TWP, SAFER, and CAGNN use a two-la<sub>y</sub>er SGC [41] with 256-dim hidden units as the backbone (frozen after <sub>p</sub>re-trainin<sub>g</sub> in TPP [25]), since TAAM i<sub>s</sub> b<sub>u</sub>ilt <sub>on a</sub> f<sub>rozen</sub> SGC <sub>an</sub>d <sub>s</sub>t<sub>ronger</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>ones wou</sub>ld <sub>con</sub>f<sub>oun</sub>d <sub>s</sub>tr<sub>a</sub>t<sub>egy w</sub>ith <sub>capac</sub>it<sub>y.</sub> TWP<sub>,</sub> SAFER<sub>, a</sub>nd CAGNN k<sub>eep</sub> th<sub>e</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>kbones their methods are defined on. TWP [20] <sub>p</sub>enalizes chan<sub>g</sub>es to the <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h attention network (GAT) [39] <sub>p</sub>arameters that are im<sub>p</sub>or tant for its loss and its attention-based a<sub>gg</sub>re<sub>g</sub>ation. CAGNN [42] uses its own dendritic <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h convolutions. SAFER [45] trains task modules on a two-la<sub>y</sub>er <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h convolutional network (GCN) [16] that is <sub>p</sub>re-trained with GRACE [54] on the first task and then f<sub>rozen.</sub> E<sub>ac</sub>h <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>ese</sub> b<sub>ac</sub>kb<sub>ones a</sub>dd<sub>s an ac</sub>ti<sub>va</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> b<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ween</sub> it<sub>s</sub> <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h convol<sub>u</sub>tions<sub>,</sub> so kee<sub>p</sub>in<sub>g</sub> them does not favor CoPE-CGL.

F<sub>o</sub>r C<sub>o</sub>PE-CGL<sub>, we</sub> tr<sub>a</sub>in f<sub>o</sub>r 500 <sub>epoc</sub>h<sub>s.</sub> All m<sub>e</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s</sub> in T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>es</sub> 2 and 3 and the scalabilit<sub>y</sub> stud<sub>y</sub> (Sec. 4.6) are run and timed with the same scri<sub>p</sub>t on one NVIDIA A100 (80GB) server with P<sub>y</sub>Torch 2<sub>.</sub>4<sub>.</sub>1<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e no</sub>i<sub>se an</sub>d <sub>run</sub>ti<sub>me-</sub>b<sub>rea</sub>kd<sub>own exper</sub>i<sub>men</sub>t<sub>s are run on an</sub> NVIDIA RTX A4000<sub>, a</sub>nd <sub>so</sub>m<sub>e</sub> r<sub>ows o</sub>f th<sub>e equa</sub>l-m<sub>e</sub>m<sub>o</sub>r<sub>y co</sub>m<sub>pa</sub>rison (Sec. 4.3) are run on an A100, so runnin<sub>g</sub> times are com<sub>p</sub>ared <sub>on</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>among</sub> <sub>me</sub>th<sub>o</sub>d<sub>s</sub> i<sub>n</sub> th<sub>e</sub> <sub>same</sub> t<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> <sub>or</sub> fi<sub>gure.</sub> R<sub>ep</sub>l<sub>ay-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d b<sub>ase</sub>li<sub>nes</sub> <sub>use</sub> <sub>per-</sub>t<sub>as</sub>k <sub>memory</sub> b<sub>u</sub>d<sub>ge</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>o</sub>f 100<sub>,</sub> 200<sub>,</sub> 300<sub>,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d 400 <sub>no</sub>d<sub>es on</sub> C<sub>ora</sub>F<sub>u</sub>ll<sub>,</sub> A<sub>rx</sub>i<sub>v,</sub> R<sub>e</sub>ddit<sub>, an</sub>d P<sub>ro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t<sub>s, an</sub>d 100<sub>,</sub> 200<sub>, an</sub>d 300 nodes on Kindle, DBLP, and ACM (4,000 nodes in total on Arxiv after 20 tasks). Codebase URLs are listed in A<sub>pp</sub>endix A.2.

## 4.4 Routing Accuracy and Mixed-Task Test Graphs

4.4.1 Correlated Routing Errors and Task-ID Accuracy. ## 4.5 Robustness to Graph Structure

Noisy edges. Bot<sup>h</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e smoot<sup>h</sup>ing operator and t<sup>h</sup>e communities of CoPE-CGL are com<sub>p</sub>uted from observed ed<sub>g</sub>es<sub>,</sub> so ed<sub>g</sub>e noise p<sup>ro</sup>p<sup>a</sup>g<sup>ates</sup> <sup>into</sup> <sup>its</sup> <sup>features</sup>, p<sup>rotot</sup>yp<sup>es</sup>, <sup>and</sup> <sup>routin</sup>g<sup>.</sup> <sup>To</sup> <sup>ever</sup>y <sup>task</sup> <sub>grap</sub>h <sub>o</sub>f t<sub>wo</sub> t<sub>rans</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> <sub>an</sub>d t<sub>wo</sub> i<sub>n</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>ti<sub>ve</sub> b<sub>enc</sub>h<sub>mar</sub>k<sub>s,</sub> <sub>we</sub> <sub>a</sub>dd <sub>un</sub>if<sub>orm</sub>l<sub>y</sub> <sub>ran</sub>d<sub>om</sub> <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e</sub> <sub>pa</sub>i<sub>rs</sub> <sub>as</sub> <sub>un</sub>di<sub>rec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges,</sub> <sub>num</sub>b<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> 10<sub>–</sub> 50% <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>a</sub>t <sub>grap</sub>h’<sub>s e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> li<sub>s</sub>t<sub>, an</sub>d <sub>compare w</sub>ith th<sub>e rep</sub>l<sub>ay-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> baselines TPP, TAAM, SAFER, and CAGNN (Table 9). ## 5 RELATED WORK

Graph data systems and model maintenance. Grap<sup>h</sup> processing s<sub>y</sub>stems mana<sub>g</sub>e lar<sub>g</sub>e, evolvin<sub>g</sub> <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs [32], and <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h streamin<sub>g</sub> f<sub>ramewor</sub>k<sub>s app</sub>l<sub>y e</sub>d<sub>ge</sub> i<sub>nser</sub>ti<sub>ons an</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>e</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e s</sub>t<sub>ore</sub>d <sub>grap</sub>h as the<sub>y</sub> arrive [2]. S<sub>y</sub>stems for learnin<sub>g</sub> on such <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs train tem-<sub>p</sub>oral GNNs on billion-scale <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs [53], train d<sub>y</sub>namic GNNs over slidin<sub>g</sub> windows of a <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h stream [5], u<sub>p</sub>date node re<sub>p</sub>resentations incrementall<sub>y</sub> in a distributed dataflow [12], decou<sub>p</sub>le <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h <sub>p</sub>ro<sub>p</sub>a-<sub>g</sub>ation from <sub>p</sub>rediction on lar<sub>g</sub>e d<sub>y</sub>namic <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs [51], or com<sub>p</sub>ress the <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h so that GNN inference runs on a smaller one [9]. These s<sub>y</sub>stems kee<sub>p</sub> the evolvin<sub>g</sub> <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h<sub>,</sub> or a com<sub>p</sub>ressed co<sub>py</sub> of it<sub>,</sub> in <sub>s</sub>t<sub>orage</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>t <sub>over</sub> <sub>a</sub> <sub>s</sub>i<sub>ng</sub>l<sub>e</sub> <sub>grap</sub>h <sub>ra</sub>th<sub>er</sub> th<sub>an</sub> <sub>over</sub> <sub>c</sub>l<sub>asses</sub> that arrive task b<sub>y</sub> task. Data mana<sub>g</sub>ement research also miti<sub>g</sub>ates the im<sub>p</sub>act of data drift on machine learnin<sub>g</sub> <sub>p</sub>i<sub>p</sub>elines [8] and mini<sub>m</sub>i<sub>zes</sub> th<sub>e</sub> d<sub>a</sub>t<sub>a co</sub>ll<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d f<sub>or mac</sub>hi<sub>ne</sub> l<sub>earn</sub>i<sub>ng over</sub> I<sub>n</sub>t<sub>erne</sub>t <sub>o</sub>f Thin<sub>g</sub>s (IoT) data streams [33]. CoPE-CGL addresses the com<sub>p</sub>le-<sub>men</sub>t<sub>ary pro</sub>bl<sub>em o</sub>f <sub>ma</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng a mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l l<sub>earne</sub>d f<sub>rom an evo</sub>l<sub>v</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub><sup>h</sup> w<sup>i</sup>t<sup>h</sup>out <sup>k</sup>ee<sub>p</sub><sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub> <sup>i</sup>ts own co<sub>py</sub> o<sup>f</sup> <sub>p</sub>ast <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub><sup>h</sup> <sup>d</sup>ata.

Continual graph learning. C<sup>l</sup>assica<sup>l</sup> continua<sup>l l</sup>earning met<sup>h</sup>- ods fall into three families: re<sub>g</sub>ularization (EWC [17], MAS [1]), memor<sub>y</sub> re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> (GEM [24]), and <sub>p</sub>arameter isolation (Pro<sub>g</sub>ressive Nets [31]). These have been extended to <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs throu<sub>g</sub>h to<sub>p</sub>olo<sub>gy</sub>- aware re<sub>g</sub>ularization (TWP [20]); re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub> of exem<sub>p</sub>lar, s<sub>p</sub>arsified, or condensed sub<sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs (ER-GNN [52], SSM [48], CaT [22], PUMA [23]); to<sub>p</sub>olo<sub>gy</sub>-aware embeddin<sub>g</sub> memor<sub>y</sub> (PDGNN [46]); and to<sub>p</sub>olo<sub>gy</sub>-aware coarsenin<sub>g</sub> (TACO [13]). Recent re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>-free frameworks use s<sub>p</sub>ectral <sub>p</sub>rofiles <sub>p</sub>lus <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h <sub>p</sub>rom<sub>p</sub>ts (TPP [25]), neural s<sub>y</sub>na<sub>p</sub>se modulators (TAAM [21]), hierarchical <sub>p</sub>arameter maskin<sub>g</sub> (CAGNN [42]), or subs<sub>p</sub>ace-aware feature resha<sub>p</sub>in<sub>g</sub> (SAFER [45]). Unlike re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>-based methods, which store raw, <sub>con</sub>d<sub>ense</sub>d<sub>,</sub> <sub>or</sub> <sub>coarsene</sub>d <sub>grap</sub>h<sub>s</sub> <sub>an</sub>d <sub>rep</sub>l<sub>ay</sub> th<sub>em</sub> i<sub>n</sub> l<sub>a</sub>t<sub>er</sub> t<sub>ra</sub>i<sub>n</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> (e.<sub>g</sub>., CaT and TACO), CoPE-CGL s<sub>p</sub>ends a constant amount of stora<sub>g</sub>e <sub>p</sub>er task on an ada<sub>p</sub>ter and at most � communit<sub>y</sub>-level <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>es<sub>;</sub> the <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>es are fixed statistics used onl<sub>y</sub> to route test <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs<sub>,</sub> never to retrain. Unlike existin<sub>g</sub> re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>-free methods<sub>,</sub> it avoids an<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>re-trained or <sub>p</sub>er-task GNN backbone<sub>,</sub> <sub>y</sub>ieldin<sub>g</sub> substantiall<sub>y</sub> lower trainin<sub>g</sub> cost (Secs. 3.4 and 4.2.1). Community detection. CoPE-CGL requires on<sup>l</sup>y a disjoint partition of nodes; we default to Louvain [3] and verif<sub>y</sub> that Leiden [38], Infoma<sub>p</sub> [30], Fast-Greed<sub>y</sub> [7], and Label Pro<sub>p</sub>a<sub>g</sub>ation [29] all <sub>p</sub>reserve CoPE-CGL’s su<sub>p</sub>eriorit<sub>y</sub> (Sec. 4.5). Parallel communit<sub>y</sub> detection and <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h clusterin<sub>g</sub> frameworks scale such <sub>p</sub>artitionin<sub>g</sub> to lar<sub>g</sub>e <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>hs [34, 43], and CoPE-CGL can use them without chan<sub>g</sub>e. Comm<sub>u</sub>nit<sub>y</sub> str<sub>u</sub>ct<sub>u</sub>re has also been <sub>u</sub>sed in static GRL via Communit<sub>y</sub>-as-a-Node abstractions [27, 28] and multi-level GRL [19]; unlike these reusable hierarchical re<sub>p</sub>resentations, CoPE-CGL <sub>p</sub>artitions each task <sub>g</sub>ra<sub>p</sub>h and distills the result into at most � <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>es <sub>p</sub>er task for re<sub>p</sub>la<sub>y</sub>-free task identification.

## A DATASET AND IMPLEMENTATION DETAILS

## A.1 Dataset Details

This a<sub>pp</sub>endix ex<sub>p</sub>ands on the benchmarks s<sub>u</sub>mmarized in Section 4.1 b<sub>y g</sub>ivin<sub>g p</sub>er-dataset feature and construction details. Perdataset statistics are re<sub>p</sub>orted in Table 1.

Transductive benchmarks (CGLB protocol [47]). CoraFull and Arxiv are citation networ<sup>k</sup>s wit<sup>h</sup> 70 and 40 c<sup>l</sup>asses, respective<sup>l</sup>y; C<sub>ora</sub>F<sub>u</sub>ll’<sub>s</sub> <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> <sub>are</sub> b<sub>ag-o</sub>f<sub>-wor</sub>d<sub>s</sub> <sub>vec</sub>t<sub>ors</sub> <sub>o</sub>f <sub>paper</sub> <sub>a</sub>b<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t<sub>s,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d A<sub>rx</sub>i<sub>v</sub>’<sub>s</sub> <sub>no</sub>d<sub>e</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>average</sub>d <sub>wor</sub>d <sub>em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ngs</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> paper tit<sup>l</sup>es and abstracts. Reddit is a socia<sup>l</sup>-networ<sup>k</sup> grap<sup>h</sup> in <sub>w</sub>hi<sub>c</sub>h <sub>no</sub>d<sub>es</sub> <sub>are</sub> <sub>pos</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>groupe</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>su</sub>b<sub>re</sub>ddit <sub>commun</sub>it<sub>y,</sub> <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> <sub>represen</sub>t <sub>s</sub>h<sub>are</sub>d <sub>commen</sub>t<sub>ers,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> <sub>are</sub> d<sub>er</sub>i<sub>ve</sub>d f<sub>rom</sub> <sub>pos</sub>t metadata. Products is an Amazon co-purc<sup>h</sup>asing networ<sup>k</sup> in w<sup>h</sup>ic<sup>h</sup> <sub>no</sub>d<sub>es</sub> <sub>are</sub> it<sub>ems,</sub> <sub>e</sub>d<sub>ges</sub> <sub>are</sub> f<sub>requen</sub>t <sub>co-purc</sub>h<sub>ases,</sub> <sub>an</sub>d f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ures</sub> <sub>are</sub> di<sub>mens</sub>i<sub>ona</sub>lit<sub>y-re</sub>d<sub>uce</sub>d b<sub>ag-o</sub>f<sub>-wor</sub>d<sub>s represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>ons</sub> f<sub>rom pro</sub>d<sub>uc</sub>t descri<sub>p</sub>tions.

## A.2 Implementation Details

Evaluation metrics. Average Per<sup>f</sup>ormance (AP↑) measures overa<sup>ll</sup> accurac<sub>y</sub> after all tasks; Backward Transfer (BWT↑) measures how much trainin<sub>g</sub> on later tasks im<sub>p</sub>acts earlier ones (hi<sub>g</sub>her is better):

$$
\mathrm { A P } = \frac { 1 } { T } \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { T } a _ { T , j } , \qquad \mathrm { B W T } = \frac { 1 } { T - 1 } \sum _ { j = 1 } ^ { T - 1 } ( a _ { T , j } - a _ { j , j } ) ,
$$

## B ALGORITHM

Al<sub>g</sub>orithm 1 details the trainin<sub>g</sub> and inference <sub>p</sub>rocedures of CoPE-CGL. The frame<sub>w</sub>ork o<sub>p</sub>erates in t<sub>w</sub>o <sub>p</sub>hases: contin<sub>u</sub>al trainin<sub>g</sub> wit<sup>h</sup> parameter-<sup>f</sup>ree projection an<sup>d</sup> tas<sup>k</sup>-<sup>f</sup>ree in<sup>f</sup>erence via <sup>d</sup>ynamic rout<sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub>.

Phase 1: Continual training. For eac<sup>h</sup> incoming tas<sup>k</sup> �, t<sup>h</sup>e <sup>f</sup>ramewor<sup>k fi</sup>rst computes t<sup>h</sup>e symmetric norma<sup>l</sup>ize<sup>d</sup> a<sup>d</sup>jacency matrix $\hat { A } _ { t }$ with self-loo<sub>p</sub>s and <sub>p</sub>erforms static �-ho<sub>p</sub> La<sub>p</sub>lacian smoothin<sub>g</sub> to <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>t th<sub>e parame</sub>t<sub>er-</sub>f<sub>ree</sub> b<sub>ase em</sub>b<sub>e</sub>ddi<sub>ng</sub> $Z _ { b a s e }$ (lines 4–6). A task <sub>spec</sub>ifi<sub>c</sub> b<sub>o</sub>ttl<sub>enec</sub>k <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>er</sub> $\theta ^ { t }$ <sub>an</sub>d <sub>c</sub>l<sub>ass</sub>ifi<sub>ca</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> h<sub>ea</sub>d $\phi ^ { t }$ <sub>are</sub> th<sub>en</sub> initia<sup>l</sup>ize<sup>d</sup>, wit<sup>h</sup> t<sup>h</sup>e a<sup>d</sup>apter<sup>’</sup>s up-projection <sup>l</sup>ayer zero-initia<sup>l</sup>ize<sup>d</sup> <sub>so</sub> th<sub>a</sub>t $Z _ { a d a p t e d } = Z _ { b a s e }$ at the start of trainin<sub>g</sub> (line 7). ## C.2 Proof of Proposition 2 (Within-Task Approximation)

Let $z _ { v } = Z _ { b a s e } ^ { ( v ) }$ <sub>,</sub> l<sub>e</sub>t $c _ { m } ^ { t r }$ be the trainin<sub>g</sub> nodes of communit<sub>y</sub> $c _ { m } \in C _ { : }$ which <sub>p</sub>artition the node set � over which the <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>es are avera<sub>g</sub>e<sup>d</sup>, $\begin{array} { r } { w _ { m } = | c _ { m } ^ { t r } | / | S | , P _ { m } = \frac { 1 } { | c _ { m } ^ { t r } | } \sum _ { v \in c _ { m } ^ { t r } } z _ { v } , } \end{array}$ <sub>an</sub>d $\begin{array} { r } { P _ { 0 } = \frac { 1 } { | S | } \sum _ { v \in S } z _ { v } = } \end{array}$ $\begin{array} { r } { \sum _ { m } w _ { m } P _ { m } } \end{array}$ . Write $\bar { x } = x / \| x \| _ { 2 }$ $x \neq 0 ,$ <sub>,</sub> l<sub>e</sub>t 0<sup>¯</sup> b<sub>e any un</sub>it <sub>vec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> (then both sides of (10) are 0 when $z _ { v } = 0 ,$ <sub>, an</sub>d $P ^ { \top } p = 0$ <sup>f</sup>or every � <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en</sub> $P = 0 ,$ , so the ste<sub>p</sub>s below hold unchan<sub>g</sub>ed), and let $a ( \upsilon )$ b<sub>e</sub> th<sub>e co</sub>mm<sub>u</sub>nit<sub>y co</sub>nt<sub>a</sub>inin<sub>g �.</sub> F<sub>o</sub>r <sub>a</sub>n<sub>y u</sub>nit <sub>vec</sub>t<sub>o</sub>r ${ \boldsymbol { \mathit { p } } } ,$

$$
\| z _ { v } \| _ { 2 } \| \bar { z } _ { v } - p \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } = 2 \| z _ { v } \| _ { 2 } - 2 z _ { v } ^ { \top } p .\tag{10}
$$

Avera<sub>g</sub>in<sub>g</sub> (10) over � with the <sub>g</sub>lobal <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>e <sub>g</sub>ives

$$
\mathbb { E } _ { v } \big [ \| z _ { v } \| _ { 2 } \| \bar { z } _ { v } - \bar { P } _ { 0 } \| _ { 2 } ^ { 2 } \big ] = 2 \mathbb { E } _ { v } \| z _ { v } \| _ { 2 } - 2 \| P _ { 0 } \| _ { 2 } ,\tag{11}
$$

and with each node’s own communit<sub>y</sub> <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>e

$$

(i) TPP and TAAM [21, 25] also com<sub>p</sub>ute <sub>p</sub>arameter-free <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>es, but one <sub>p</sub>er task from a<sup>ll</sup> tra<sup>i</sup>n<sup>i</sup>n<sub>g</sub> no<sup>d</sup>es; our <sub>p</sub>rotot<sub>yp</sub>es are com<sub>p</sub>ute<sup>d</sup> <sub>p</sub>er commun<sup>i</sup>t<sub>y</sub> on th<sub>e same</sub> $Z _ { b a s e }$ th<sub>a</sub>t th<sub>e a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>ers use, so rou</sub>ti<sub>ng an</sub>d <sub>a</sub>d<sub>ap</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on s</sub>h<sub>are</sub> one embedding. (ii) The multi-prototype set provides a granular d<sub>ens</sub>it<sub>y</sub> <sub>es</sub>ti<sub>ma</sub>t<sub>e</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k’<sub>s</sub> f<sub>ea</sub>t<sub>ure</sub> <sub>space,</sub> <sub>so</sub> <sub>quer</sub>i<sub>es</sub> d<sub>rawn</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> di<sub>verse</sub> <sub>ne</sub>i<sub>g</sub>hb<sub>or</sub>h<sub>oo</sub>d<sub>s</sub> <sub>can</sub> <sub>ma</sub>t<sub>c</sub>h <sub>any</sub> <sub>o</sub>f th<sub>e</sub> t<sub>as</sub>k’<sub>s</sub> l<sub>oca</sub>l <sub>mo</sub>d<sub>es</sub> rather than a sin<sub>g</sub>le avera<sub>g</sub>ed centroid (em<sub>p</sub>iricall<sub>y</sub> validated in the task-ID accurac<sub>y</sub> anal<sub>y</sub>sis in Section 4.4).
